<details>
<summary><strong>Prompt</strong> — Use @[char ref] for the potter's identity, face, amber eyes, blue-black hair cut blunt at the jaw with the short copper-bound braid at her right temple, the fine gold kintsugi...</summary>

~~~~text
Use @[char ref] for the potter's identity, face, amber eyes, blue-black hair cut blunt at the jaw with the short copper-bound braid at her right temple, the fine gold kintsugi seams on her left cheek and chest, the white porcelain right forearm and hand joined by fine gold lacquer, the long open indigo wrap robe over cream wrapped linen, the wide brown leather belt hung with small steel tools, and her bare feet with grey clay dust dried across the shins. Preserve her proportions. Ignore the sheet's backdrop and repeated views. Invent no accessories; the only objects are the wheel, the clay and the water basin.

Cinematic 2D anime with clean linework, cel shading and painted surfaces. A dim workshop: plank walls, shelves of unfired pots receding into shadow, a water basin on the bench, a cool blue window behind her and one warm lamp hanging low at screen-left. Amber key against blue shadow. Wet clay holds a dull sheen; the porcelain forearm reads matte against skin; the gold seams catch the lamp and flare.

Format: 15 seconds, 9:16 vertical, 3 shots joined by hard cuts, real time, no slow motion, no dialogue.

Geography lock: she sits behind the wheel facing camera, the lamp always at screen-left, the window always behind her, the basin at screen-right on the bench. The camera stays on the near side of the wheel and never crosses behind her. The clay sits at the centre of the wheel head for the whole film.

Mechanics: the wheel turns continuously and never stops. Her skin hand works the outside of the form, the porcelain hand the inside. The clay obeys pressure: it rises where the hands narrow, thins as it climbs, and once the wall is too thin it cannot recover, so it leans and folds rather than springing back. One lump of clay throughout, never replaced.

Camera grammar: one move per shot, coupled to the clay. Push in as the form rises, hold still when she reacts, push again as she restarts. Keep the wheel head and her hands in frame together during work, and keep the whole face in frame during the reaction. Brake briefly on the collapse so it reads.

0-3s, shot 1: Close three-quarter from just above the wheel, looking down across her forearms to the clay. The grey column turns fast between her hands; the wall lifts and narrows as her palms close. Clay slicks the gold seams in the porcelain. The camera pushes in slowly.

3-5.5s: Continue the same shot. The wall climbs taller and thinner, ribbing into fine spiral ridges. A fleck of clay flicks off the rim and crosses the lamp beam. The tools at her belt sway with her breathing.

5.5-8s: Continue. The rim begins to waver, tips off centre, and the wall folds over on itself and settles into a low collapsed spiral. Her hands lift clear and hold above it, fingers open. Cut on the hands stopping.

8-11.5s, shot 2: Hard cut to a locked close-up of her face, lamp hard from the left, window light rimming her jaw. She looks down at the ruin and her jaw sets. The gold seam on her cheek flares in the lamp. Her shoulders drop, breath goes out, and the corner of her mouth lifts into a small closed-lip smile. The copper-bound braid swings once against her neck. Cut on the smile arriving.

11.5-15s, shot 3: Hard cut to a low angle just above the wheel head, her hands entering from above. The porcelain hand presses the collapsed spiral down into a rounded mound and holds it centred while her other hand drives the wheel edge and the wheel accelerates. Her thumb sinks and opens a shallow new bowl, and the clay runs true again. The camera pushes in slowly. End on the clay turning, no held pose, no further cut.

Sound: the wheel's steady electric hum throughout, the low rumble of turning clay, water trickling in the basin, the soft heavy fold of the collapse, one quiet breath out, the creak of the leather belt, and the wheel rising to a faster whir at the end. No music. No subtitles, captions, logos, title cards or overlays.
~~~~

</details>

</a>

<details>
<summary><strong>Prompt</strong> — 15s | 16:9 | One Continuous Take | Korean Dialogue Only | No BGM/Subtitles Ultra-photorealistic live-action supernatural martial-arts thriller. So-Yeon (same actress throughout):...</summary>

~~~~text
15s | 16:9 | One Continuous Take | Korean Dialogue Only | No BGM/Subtitles

Ultra-photorealistic live-action supernatural martial-arts thriller. So-Yeon (same actress throughout): 160cm, messy black hair covering eyes, black hoodie, gray sweatpants, Converse. Real human actors, real locations, practical VFX only.

Location: Rain-soaked Korean alley behind a night market. Food cart, scooters, crates, puddles, shutters, signs, cardboard boxes.

Combat Style: Unarmed only. Sanda + Muay Thai clinch + Silat. Fast, messy, close-quarters fighting. Multiple attackers, realistic collisions, no poses or theatrical choreography.

Fault-Line Physics: Strength remains physical and grounded. Heavy impacts create realistic cracks from exact contact points: impact → vibration → cracks → dust/water movement → debris settling. No glow, energy effects, shockwaves, teleportation, or magic visuals.

0–3s — Blocked Alley

So-Yeon walks carrying a small plastic bag. Seven thugs block the alley. A delivery rider tries to pass. The leader shoves the scooter aside:

"꺼져."

So-Yeon steps forward:

"그 사람 건드리지 마."

The leader grabs her shoulder.

3–7s — First Collision

She counters instantly: wrist control → knee → elbow → throw. He crashes into stacked crates. Her sneaker lands in a puddle; thin cracks spread through the pavement. Everyone freezes.

A thug yells:

"다 같이 가!"

All six rush her.

7–11s — Market Chaos

She ducks a punch that hits a metal shutter, redirects attackers into each other, throws one into a food cart, and sweeps another. The delivery rider pulls the elderly shop owner out of danger. Brief 0.2s micro slow-motion on a heavy impact as cracks spread through wet pavement before returning to real time.

11–15s — Human Moment

The last two attackers collide into a concrete wall, creating a deep crack. Silence.

The rider asks:

"누나... 괜찮아요?"

So-Yeon looks at her trembling hands. The elderly shop owner returns her dropped plastic bag:

"이거."

She accepts it with a small embarrassed bow. The damaged cart slowly rolls in the background.

CUT TO BLACK.

Camera: Reactive handheld documentary style, tight dialogue close-ups, sudden reframing during action, natural autofocus hunting, realistic motion blur, impact shake, authentic human reactions. No superhero poses, no exaggerated expressions, no CGI doubles. Focus equally on civilians, environment, and grounded physical combat.
~~~~

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Prompt</strong> — Hyper-kinetic action-film title sequence opening on a mirror-polished titanium wall in complete darkness. A razor-thin energy cut suddenly slices through the surface, spraying...</summary>

~~~~text
Hyper-kinetic action-film title sequence opening on a mirror-polished titanium wall in complete darkness. A razor-thin energy cut suddenly slices through the surface, spraying white-hot sparks and molten droplets across the frame. A second cut appears from another angle, then dozens strike in rapid succession, carving deep geometric paths through the metal. The camera aggressively follows each slash as huge titanium sections separate and spin through the air. A powerful magnetic field violently pulls the fragments toward the center, where sharpened metal plates slide, rotate and lock together to construct "BLADE" with surgical precision. The completed title is thin, lethal and forged from scratched black steel, every edge glowing red-hot from friction. The camera sweeps inches above the letters before one final invisible slash cuts through the entire word and triggers a spectacular explosion of metal splinters and sparks. Aggressive, precise, dark, premium Hollywood action typography.
~~~~

</details>

## 4. She found a cell phone that had been secretly filming her, and the camera was right behind her.

<a href="https://media.beatapi.io/prompt-gallery/minimax-h3/she-found-a-cell-phone-that-had-been-secretly-filming-584121/video-c785f0871a16.webm">
  <img src="https://media.beatapi.io/prompt-gallery/minimax-h3/she-found-a-cell-phone-that-had-been-secretly-filming-584121/poster-b585bc27a35d.jpg" alt="She found a cell phone that had been secretly filming her, and the camera was right behind her. video preview" width="700" />
</a>

<details>
<summary><strong>Prompt</strong> — For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, &lt;Picture 1&gt; (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced. integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] A 15-second, 16:9...</summary>

~~~~text
For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, <Picture 1> (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced.

integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] A 15-second, 16:9 cinematic realistic suspense short set entirely inside the same rainy neon stair alley from <Picture 1>. Preserve the exact same female lead throughout: same face, hairstyle, soaked wardrobe, body proportions, skin texture, and the same staircase geography with wet steps, narrow alley, railings, humid air, and neon mood. No scene change, no extra people, no black ending. The story is: a drenched woman slowly walks down the stairs, notices an iPhone still recording on one step, bends to pick it up, realizes the screen shows her own back filmed from behind, instinctively steps back half a step into the railing and whips her shoulder and head around, but the real staircase is empty. End on the suspended moment where she is half-turned, half-looking down, with the screen still lit. She stays in motion throughout: descending, stopping, noticing the phone, bending to pick it up, standing with the screen, stepping back, leaning to the railing, turning shoulder and head, then looking back to the phone. The phone must stay physically consistent in size and readability. [Shot 2] At 00:00-00:02.8, a low dynamic near shot hugs her legs, waist, and the wet railing as the camera slowly pushes in and follows her down about one and a half steps. The emphasis is on lower-body weight transfer, wet skin reflections, and action-based sensual tension. Her right hand keeps a subtle stabilizing relationship with the railing. She is soaked, moving carefully but not weakly. Her first emotional phase is calm, restrained, and oppressive self-protection: eyelids half-lowered, gaze angled beyond camera toward the lower stairs, brows held flat with only the outer ends slightly lowered, lips naturally parted, breath low and steady, nostrils moving lightly, no smile. Pink-violet neon washes the front-upper planes of the chest, collarbone, abdomen, and front thighs, while cyan-blue edge light wraps the waistline, outer arms, and leg contour. This is the first sensual close-up: legs, waist, wet skin, deep wet fabric contours, and real descending balance. [Shot 3] At 00:02.8-00:05.0, cut to a medium-close slightly high angle that lands on the step at her feet while keeping one stopped boot and the railing in the foreground. A lit iPhone lies near the edge of shallow step-water. The red recording dot must be immediately readable. She stops on the stair, not frozen but caught mid-motion. Her gaze narrows sharply toward the phone, pupils snap into focus, the brow center contracts into only the thinnest line, she takes one short nasal breath, her lips close lightly from their previously open state, and the jaw gathers in. This is the first layer of wrongness, not yet fear. She murmurs quietly, <d>[English] Whose phone is this...?</d> The line is tentative and low, with the ending gently falling away. Phone screen light adds a third small light source, lifting the forehead edge, nose bridge, lash line, and cupid’s bow with cool white. [Shot 4] At 00:05.0-00:07.8, a tight near shot from her right-front follows her bend as she picks up the phone. Stay close to the hand, collarbone, side waist, wet hair falling forward, and the exact moment the device leaves the stair. She remains connected to the railing with the right side of her body and hand relationship, even while reaching. Her eyes stay locked on the phone the whole time. Lashes cast a slight shadow downward. The lips tighten more, the throat moves with one very small swallow, breathing becomes noticeably shallower, and the face shifts into focused verification rather than panic. This is the second sensual close-up: hand, collarbone, side waist, wet hair, and the phone entering the center of the narrative. Rain hits the glass and forms tiny round splashes and sliding beads. When she pinches the phone edge and lifts it, droplets drag aside and one or two thin water threads stretch briefly and break. [Shot 5] At 00:07.8-00:10.5, a near shot layers the phone screen and half her face. First make the screen content legible, then carry focus into her face without losing the screen read. The phone display shows only one clear thing: her own back descending the staircase, filmed from a steady hidden angle one step higher behind her. No second face, no ghost, no supernatural clutter. She sees it and recognizes herself. Her fourth emotional phase is clear alert recognition: her eyes flick once across the screen and then hold, the lower lids lift slightly, the pupils widen just a little, the brow center tightens more clearly while still restrained, nostrils open and settle, any remaining softness in the mouth disappears, and the lower lip stalls for a beat as if a sentence is trapped in the throat. She whispers, <d>[English] Wait... that’s me.</d> The first half carries a tiny delay as recognition catches in her breath. The phone’s cold light now fills the lower half of her face and under-eye area, making the micro-expression readable without overpowering the neon mood. [Shot 6] At 00:10.5-00:12.8, an extreme close-up pushes from shoulder-neck, side face, lashes, and lip line into the eye. This is the third sensual close-up, centered on the dangerous moment before she turns. She keeps the phone close to her chest line, glances once more at the screen, and then prepares to look back. Her fifth emotional phase is being pressed by the implication that the filming angle was behind her: lashes tremble once, breathing tightens, the lip line draws in, cheek muscles firm slightly, and she says in a lower, faster, more pressured whisper, <d>[English] Who the hell is filming me?</d> It is not shouted, but forced low confirmation. One side of her face drops deeper into shadow while the other is caught by soft pink-violet neon on lashes, nose tip, lip peak, and jaw edge. [Shot 7] At 00:12.8-00:15.0, a medium-close shot with a slight retreat. Let the turning motion carry the cut. She shifts back by half a step, the wet sole dragging a very small water trace, shoulder and collarbone leading the head turn rather than the head teleporting first. Her back and right side find the railing. She turns sharply toward the upper stairs, but the real staircase is empty. She then drops her gaze back to the still-lit phone at chest level. Her final emotional phase is cold controlled aftershock: the eyes cut first upward, then back down, the forehead tightens lightly, the jaw stays tight, the mouth opens by half a breath and then slowly closes again, fear controlled but not gone. The screen remains lit and still recording. The staircase behind her stays empty, wet, neon-lit, and physically real. End with her half-turned, half-looking down at the glowing iPhone, rain still falling, neon still flickering, and suspense held inside the frame, without cutting to black.

overall_soundscape: Maintain a continuous rainy neon stairwell ambience: mid-strength rain, water ticking on railing and steps, shallow drainage flow, distant electric hum, soft neon buzz, and humid night reverb. Emphasize wet footfalls on the stairs, the subtle grip and slide of her hand on the railing, clothing friction, low controlled breath, and fine splashes from shallow step-water. Then bring forward rain striking the phone glass, tiny circular water impacts, the slight stop in her step, her low tentative line <d>[English] Whose phone is this...?</d>, fingers contacting the wet phone, water dragging across the screen, a faint lift-off suction from the wet stair, her shallow breath, fabric tension as she bends, one small swallow, and the surrounding rain and neon hum dimming slightly as she reads the screen. Finish with the tightening breath before the turn, the low pressured line <d>[English] Who the hell is filming me?</d>, one light metallic touch from the railing, the small backward step drag on wet stair, empty stair ambience, continuing rain, neon buzz, and the phone still recording in her hand. No scream, no extra voices, no subtitles.

non_diegetic_music: Keep score absent or nearly absent. If any non-diegetic layer is used, make it only a very thin low suspense drone under the rain and neon hum, rising slightly after she sees the screen and never overpowering the physical environment or dialogue.
~~~~

</details>

## 5. 📷“Wake Up, Neon”

<a href="https://media.beatapi.io/prompt-gallery/minimax-h3/wake-up-neon-236692/video-664bc4a0fa7b.webm">
  <img src="https://media.beatapi.io/prompt-gallery/minimax-h3/wake-up-neon-236692/poster-7f0a819f42d0.jpg" alt="📷“Wake Up, Neon” video preview" width="700" />
</a>

<details>
<summary><strong>Prompt</strong> — For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, &lt;Picture 1&gt; (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced. integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] A 15-second, 16:9...</summary>

~~~~text
For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, <Picture 1> (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced.

integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] A 15-second, 16:9 cinematic cyber-thriller image-to-video sequence set entirely inside the same wet neon back alley from <Picture 1>: the left-front railing zone where A begins, the street centerline, the puddle reflection layer, and the opposite fog-filled exit in the background. Do not add extra people, do not place both women on the same staircase segment, and do not leave this alley. Subject A is the blue-haired woman from <Picture 1>, with the same face, body, black deep-V leather bodysuit, fishnets, over-knee boots, metallic choker, glowing beverage can, goggles, and jacket ring-light. Subject B must be the exact same face, hairstyle, body, and clothing silhouette as A, but with a refined dangerous harlequin-style makeup variation, not a different woman. The story is: A rules her own staircase entrance like a queen, fog grows at the opposite exit, a Queen of Hearts card flies out, A catches it one-handed and recognizes it as a provocation, then rises and looks up to see her identical mirror-woman emerging from the fog and forcing her to leave her throne. Lighting comes only from practical neon, staircase reflections, the fog backlight, and distant storefront light. Rain, wet hair, cloth folds, skin reflections, boot splashes, condensation droplets, and natural body inertia remain physically real. [Shot 2] At 00:00-00:03.5, begin with an extremely low ground-level push from puddles, discarded paper, and lane markings into A’s boots, fishnets, thighs, leather chest, and jaw, ending in a near frame from chest to lips. A sits on the center of the third step from the bottom of the staircase, leaning slightly forward as if guarding the entrance to her territory. This is the first sensual close-up, built from collarbone, throat line, deep-V leather highlights, and the nearly invisible rise and fall of her breathing. Her first emotional state is cool sovereign control: brows relaxed but not soft, eyelids half-lowered, gaze not fully locked on the distance yet, lips lightly closed, breathing almost silent, throat moving once faintly. The puddles reflect blue and magenta neon steadily, and the opposite street exit holds only a thin fog presence. [Shot 3] At 00:03.5-00:07.0, the camera eases slightly backward and rises just enough to include both the staircase foreground and the opposite exit. The fog there becomes denser, first rolling close to the ground and then pressing outward slowly, with neon cutting bright lines along its edges. A is still seated, but the second emotional stage begins. She does not widen her eyes dramatically. Instead her breathing stops for half a beat first, her lips loosen by a fraction, the lower lids lift slightly, and her gaze truly focuses toward the opposite exit for the first time. Her head tips subtly toward the source of the sound and fog. Then a Queen of Hearts card is carried out of the fog by damp wind, turning half a rotation and gliding naturally through the mid-ground mist toward A. Do not chase the card wildly. A’s right hand rises fast and clean and catches it one-handed at chest height slightly to her right, with real deceleration in the wrist and fingers. Her third emotional stage is confirmation of provocation: before the catch, her eyes narrow sharply and the brow center hardens slightly; after the catch, she drops her gaze to the card, the throat moves once, the jawline begins to tighten, and the original calm disappears. This is the second sensual close-up, built from the catching wrist, collarbone, leather highlights across the chest, eyelash shadows, and the lip line and jaw while glancing down at the card. The Queen of Hearts face must be clearly readable; the paper is damp-soft with slightly curled edges and realistic motion. [Shot 4] At 00:07.0-00:10.2, from A’s left-front, the camera makes a slight lateral push and subtle rise, keeping the staircase, street, and opposite exit on one axis. A places her left hand on the railing and rises slowly. Her right foot steps down first, then the left follows into a high-low standing stance. She never leaves the immediate front zone of her staircase. The card remains in her right hand. Her fourth emotional stage is calm transition into combat readiness: eyes narrower and more concentrated, nostrils opening slightly with breath, lips tightening, jaw sharpening, and her gaze lifting off the card and back to the opposite exit as if thinking, “You really dared to do this.” During the rise, the leather outfit stretches and rebounds naturally, hair shifts back and settles, and her chest shows only a very slight real inertia response. This is the third sensual close-up, built from waist and hip effort, fishnet leg tension, neck and jaw line, and the chest moving with controlled breath. [Shot 5] At 00:10.2-00:12.7, hold a longer relation shot. A stays on the left side or left-lower edge of the foreground while the opposite exit sits centered in the back. Focus transfers gradually from A to B. B walks out of the fog by two small steps and stops at the edge of the backlit mist. She is the same face, same hairstyle, same body, same clothing outline as A, but with refined dangerous harlequin-style makeup: darker eye area, sharper mouth corners, and a more poisonous finish. A’s fifth emotional stage is recognition freeze. She locks for one beat: pupils contract quickly, the lip line releases for half a second, the throat catches on one breath, and for the first time her breathing goes off rhythm. Then she does not retreat; she becomes more focused. B’s emotional state is intimate contempt: stable eyes, one mouth corner rising very slowly, as if appreciating that A has finally understood. The fog keeps rolling behind B but never hides her face, and her body parts it with real wraparound flow. [Shot 6] At 00:12.7-00:15.0, use one restrained reverse shot to B in a medium close frame, pushing in extremely slowly. B stands at the street-exit edge, chin lifted slightly, one-sided smile in place, gaze locked on A at a distance. Before the line, give one tiny false-soft look; on the key words, the gaze turns straighter and the mouth shape grows more precise and cutting. The only spoken line is <d>[English] You dropped your crown.</d> It must be low, intimate, cold, and humiliating rather than loud. “You” is pressed low, “dropped” carries a little more weight, and “your crown” falls away like a verdict. After the line, A in the far frame or foreground edge takes one more step down, committing to leave her throne and meet the challenge. Her final emotional state shifts from frozen recognition into hard resolve: eyes harden again, lips tighten again, jaw stabilizes, shoulders do not collapse. When the line lands, a nearby neon briefly flickers once, and both the fog edge behind B and the puddle reflections brighten for an instant. End on the moment just before A moves toward the opposite side. Do not cut to black.

overall_soundscape: Maintain a wet cyber alley ambience throughout: neon electrical hum, distant ventilation fans, moist air resonance, occasional water drips, and low street reverb. Emphasize puddle surface noise, faint leather movement, cloth friction, the subtle sound of A shifting on the step, and her almost inaudible breath. Then bring forward the slow spill of fog, the slight change in airflow at the opposite exit, the card fluttering and rotating, the light slap of paper into A’s fingers, a fine metallic tick from the railing, leather stretch and settle as she rises, fog movement as B parts it, soft heel contacts from B’s two steps, and B’s close cold line <d>[English] You dropped your crown.</d> Afterward restore the alley ambience with neon hiss, damp air, tiny puddle ripples, and fog drift. No extra voices, no subtitle cues, no music-video stingers.

non_diegetic_music: Keep score minimal or absent. If any non-diegetic layer is used, make it a very thin low-frequency suspense bed beneath the alley ambience, rising only slightly once B is revealed and never overpowering the voice, neon, or the physical space.
~~~~

</details>

## 6. Made with MiniMax H3

  <img src="https://media.beatapi.io/prompt-gallery/minimax-h3/made-with-minimax-h3-558505/poster-20724b7a5253.jpg" alt="Made with MiniMax H3 video preview" width="700" />
</a>

<details>
<summary><strong>Prompt</strong> — Photorealistic cinematic aerial video of a modern glass skyscraper assembling itself on a city construction site. Subject: a rectangular glass office tower growing from an empty...</summary>

~~~~text
Photorealistic cinematic aerial video of a modern glass skyscraper assembling itself on a city construction site.

Subject: a rectangular glass office tower growing from an empty lot, surrounded by three yellow tower cranes. Cyan holographic construction overlays with grid lines, vertical pillars, and orbiting energy rings.

Environment: dense low-rise East Asian city block with tiled roofs, green rooftops, trees lining the streets, buses and cars on the roads, taller buildings and a sports field in the distance.

Style: ultra-realistic aerial photography, golden-hour to sunset color grade, sharp glass reflections, warm interior lights versus cool cyan holograms.

Camera: one continuous high-angle drone shot. Starts looking down on the lot, then slowly pulls back and lifts as the building grows, ending on a hero three-quarter view of the completed tower against the sunset.

Lighting: late afternoon sun at the start; sky shifts to orange, coral, and pink clouds; finished tower glows with warm yellow office lights.

[0s] Empty rectangular construction pit in the city. Three yellow cranes stand around the site. A faint cyan holographic floor grid appears on the dirt.

[2s] Glowing cyan pillars rise from the grid and lock into a structural skeleton. Cranes stay in place.

[4s] Concrete core and first floor slabs materialize. Cyan rings begin orbiting the structure. Camera starts a slow pull-back and rise.

[7s] Mid-rise floors stack rapidly. Dark glass curtain walls wrap the lower levels. Interior lights start coming on. Sky turns warmer.

[10s] Tower is now a tall glass skyscraper. Cranes lift the last rooftop mechanical sections into place. Cyan hologram fades. Sunset sky is intense orange-pink.

[13s] Construction complete. Clean modern tower with lit interiors, one remaining crane at the base. Hold the final cinematic aerial frame.

Audio: distant city traffic, compressed construction rumble early, then a low cinematic swell as the building finishes. No dialogue. No on-screen text. No watermark.

Avoid: cuts, shaky camera, cartoon look, people close-up, logos, extra towers appearing out of nowhere, watermark, subtitles, UI overlay.

#FlovaAI #CPP

A is foreground-right already rising from a one-knee hand-on-pack posture; one gloved hand presses into the ground while the other pulls a strap, making collarbone, shoulder line, chest breathing, and abdominal effort visible at once. B sits in the left mid-ground on the edge of a military crate, just finishing the second bite of an apple; her hand drops from her mouth as she turns her face slightly into the wind, pretending casualness while listening. The apple clearly shows two bites removed. C enters from the right-rear carrying an oil drum, her boot stepping into sand as the drum swings with real weight. The first sensual close-up belongs to A, built from collarbone, shoulder line, chest breathing, the glove braced on the ground, and abdominal effort inside a real rise. Wind keeps pulling hair, straps slap against bags, and tank edges and pant legs wrinkle and rebound naturally. In the far right-rear background, the motorcycle moves slowly laterally, wheels turning and suspension bobbing slightly, trailing a thin dust wake immediately blown diagonally away. [Shot 3] At 00:03-00:05, the camera makes a low short lateral move between A and B, gliding from A’s shoulder side to B’s side face and hand. B lightly wipes the trace of juice sheen at the edge of her mouth with her thumb and says in a low controlled voice, <d>[English] You hear that?</d> It is probing, not dramatic. The second sensual close-up belongs to B, built from lip line, wiping gesture, jawline, knuckles, and wind-brushed hair across her cheekbone. A does not answer; she uses the moment to pull herself fully upright by the pack strap and turn toward the crate. C continues one more step toward the drum placement. The third sensual close-up belongs to C, built from arm, shoulder-back, and waist tension while lowering the drum, with the wind pressing cloth against the body. [Shot 5] At 00:09-00:12, cut to a near shot on A from front-left at roughly thirty degrees and push in extremely slowly. A presses her palm more fully onto the crate lid, putting force through the heel of the hand; throat and jawline tighten further, and her eyes move from suspicion into confirmation. C straightens, then shifts half a step back into a side-ready stance, eyes fixed on the crate, ready to intervene. Inside the crate there is a very faint old-style electronic pulse, and the latch vibrates in a fine repeated tremor. [Shot 6] At 00:12-00:15, hold a chest-up near shot of A, camera slightly below eye level, with one last very short slow push. A remains half-crouched and leaning toward the seam of the crate. For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, <Picture 1> (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced.

integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] A 15-second, 16:9, cinematic photoreal war short based on <Picture 1>. Preserve the exact same female assassin throughout: same face, proportions, hairstyle, makeup, dress construction, body proportions, and visual texture. The entire sequence stays inside one continuous industrial brick-alley battlefield with abandoned brick walls, rusted oil drums, metal support pillars, wet concrete, and clear forward corridor depth. Lighting stays consistent: cold blue industrial ambience with warm muzzle flash, sparks, and small fire reflections. Thin smoke, dust, damp air, and gunpowder haze remain present without obscuring her face. She is cold, poised, dangerous, and extremely self-controlled, wearing a pale lavender-silver slit evening dress with metallic pearlescent sheen and high heels. Her sensuality comes only from danger, precision, breath, movement, and weapon handling, never posing. She remains in continuous motion: side-slide, wall contact, shoulder turn, pillar wrap, reload, barrel kick, diagonal advance, half-kneel stabilization, and wall-slide ending. The narrative is tactical deception: she seems pinned down, but is actually controlling the corridor while retreating and using a rolling oil drum to force the real target out from the forward depth directly in front of her. The hidden killer must never fully appear early or behind her; before the reveal, only fragmentary clues are allowed. At 00:00.000-00:01.600, open on an extreme close muzzle-flash shot. Fire, brass, and the weapon body fill the frame, then the camera slides back along the gun to her gripping hand, chest rise, collarbone, jawline, and half-side face. She fires two short bursts while side-sliding along the brick wall. Her reaction stays minimal: brows gather slightly, upper lids lower, gaze stays nailed forward, nostrils move with short breath, lips part only slightly, and the jawline remains firm. [Shot 2] At 00:01.600, move into a medium-close side-follow tracking shot as brick pillars and oil drums wipe past. She withdraws toward a brick column, taps it with her left shoulder for leverage, rotates the hips, and throws her body behind the oil drum’s side while always facing the forward corridor. Her eyes cut once toward the deep corridor as if measuring range. Brows press lower, lips flatten, side neck and shoulder tighten. Enemy rounds strike the oil drum shell, ringing the metal and throwing rust sparks. [Shot 3] At 00:03.400, deliver the dangerous close-up. A slightly low close shot rises from the hand slamming in a fresh magazine up to her lip line, nose tip, and eyes, then settles to collarbone and shoulder. Her back is against the brick pillar. She reloads cleanly, snaps the action release, and wipes soot from the edge of her lower lip with one fingertip. This beat feels dangerous, not sweet: she is reloading while waiting for prey to show itself. The outer eye lifts by the smallest fraction, then lowers; the upper eyelid loosens and tightens; one mouth corner carries the faintest bad smile; she takes one slower inhale visible in the throat and collarbone; mouth movement stays tiny and exact. She whispers, breath-close: <d>[English] Come on... show me.</d> [Shot 4] At 00:05.200, shift to a low wide-near shot emphasizing the high heels, wet reflections, dress hem, oil drum, and corridor depth. She kicks the oil drum hard, sending it rolling straight into the same forward corridor she has been watching. Instantly she turns head, shoulder, body line, gun line, and gaze into that exact forward zone. The earlier taunting vanishes at once. Brows and eyes tighten, mouth returns flat, and breathing steadies into hunter mode. Water splashes and the rolling drum echoes down the corridor. [Shot 5] At 00:06.900, the camera drops low and chases the rolling drum forward. In the foreground is the rotating barrel; in the background is the corridor end she is already targeting. Only one incomplete clue may appear at the forward cover edge: a shadow flicker, a shoe tip retracting, or a metallic corner flash. No full body reveal yet. She diagonally cuts forward by half a body width, gun held low but ready, advancing one step. Her pupils stay fixed on the corridor end, upper lids press slightly lower, jaw tucks in, lips close into a narrower line, and shoulders stay low and controlled. [Shot 6] At 00:08.600, use a slightly low medium shot from her side-front angle, keeping both her advance path and the forward cover readable. She presses one more step forward and raises the gun line at the same time. Only now is the hidden killer forced into a first full half-body reveal at the forward cover edge she has already targeted. Emphasize that the target is driven out by her control of space. She shows no surprise, only confirmation. Brows stop changing and settle flat, the gaze becomes emotionless and razor-sharp, nostril movement reduces, breathing lightens, and mouth corners remain neutral. She says coldly: <d>[English] There you are.</d> [Shot 7] At 00:10.200, keep a two-thirds body medium shot as she advances two steps and drops rapidly into a half-kneel, the camera leveling with her gun line. She raises the weapon while moving; on the second step she folds into the half-kneel and locks into a stable firing position, sight fixed on the target’s chest. Blink rate drops nearly to zero. Upper lids stay pressed, jaw tightens, lips close into a thin hard line, and breath becomes almost inaudible. [Shot 8] At 00:12.000, hold a 45-degree close medium shot that keeps her face, gun muzzle, and the target collapsing in deep background within the same frame. Do not cut to reverse. She fires one single shot only. Recoil control is clean, and the target falls backward out of the cold-lit zone. Immediately after the shot, use a brief muffled vacuum effect. Her facial response is minimal: lashes tighten slightly, the mid-face never distorts, mouth corners do not move, and there is no pleasure in the kill, only completion. Just before the shot, she says softly: <d>[English] Too late.</d> [Shot 9] At 00:13.400, finish on a slow pushing medium-close shot. Because of her earlier wound, she slides down the brick wall into a seated position, dress spread across the wet ground. Her right hand still controls the gun and keeps it pointed forward. Her left hand braces against the wet floor, fingertips pressing into the water trace. The sensuality here is not fragility but the dangerous afterglow of a threat not fully over. First a restrained exhale leaks out through nose and lips; the brow softens by a fraction; the eyes descend from full hunting focus into cold peripheral watchfulness, still aimed toward where the target fell; the lower lids show slight fatigue; finally the mouth corner lifts in the thinnest trace of satisfaction. She says low and steady: <d>[English] Now it begins.</d> Cut to black immediately after. Keep hard constraints: the audience must not see the full target before she does, the first full reveal happens only in the forward depth she has already looked at and aimed toward, and the camera must advance according to her gaze and movement direction.

overall_soundscape: Use a hollow industrial battle bed throughout: damp footsteps, rolling metal resonance, shell casings on wet concrete, bullets striking metal, action cycling, corridor echo, thin smoke movement, and restrained battlefield ambience. Emphasize near gun blast, brass, muzzle pressure, and tight breathing in the opening; shoulder impact on brick, fabric drag, oil-drum ring, and rust sparks in the side-tracking section; then close body sounds for the reload, soot wipe, slow inhale, and whispered <d>[English] Come on... show me.</d> Let the kicked drum ring hard and roll through wet echo, with only a tiny clue from the target zone before reveal. Narrow the sound field as she steps in and raises the gun, let <d>[English] There you are.</d> land dry and certain, then reduce almost everything to heels on wet ground, cloth friction, breath control, the single contained shot, brief muffled vacuum, wall-slide scrape, and the final low delivery of <d>[English] Now it begins.</d>

non_diegetic_music: Keep score minimal and tension-based, using only a low restrained pulse under the industrial ambience. Let it sit almost invisibly under the first half, increase pressure slightly during the reload taunt and barrel kick, then thin again during the reveal so spatial logic and weapon sounds dominate. After the kill shot, let the score recede into a cold low note, leaving the ending carried mainly by breath, wet room tone, and controlled aftermath.

~~~~text
For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, <Picture 1> (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced.

integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] A 15-second, 16:9, cinematic photoreal war-action short based on <Picture 1>, unfolding in one single continuous battlefield space centered on the abandoned armored vehicle roof and side. Keep the space physically readable at all times: hot steel, hatch, side fireline, dust, sparks, smoke, heat shimmer, metallic debris, and nearby fire reflections. Preserve the same adult female warrior: messy blonde hair, realistic sweat, dust, light abrasions, black cropped leather jacket, tactical thigh rig, tall boots, knee pads, and stable identity. Her sensuality is mature and restrained, coming only from body control, breathing, collarbone line, side face, and gaze. Use a grounded pseudo-one-take with hidden cuts, close handheld pursuit, and low gliding camera movement orbiting her relation to the vehicle surface, hatch, and side line. At 00:00.000-00:02.300, the camera glides ultra-low across the armored roof as rust, shell casings, dust, and sparks streak past. Her left palm slams onto the burning steel while her right hand carries the rifle as she cuts diagonally forward, slides on one knee, snaps the rifle up, and fires two short controlled bursts. She never freezes: she keeps pressing forward, borrowing leverage from the vehicle and shifting weight in one continuous chain. [Shot 2] At 00:02.300, the camera slides from the hand on steel past the thigh holster and engaged waist to the collarbone, neck, and half-side face. Firelight moves across her neck and jaw. Heat shimmer lifts hair strands near her lips. She never looks at the camera. Her eyes tighten first, then rise into aim. Sweat and dust hold the lashes together slightly. Brow movement stays minimal; the lower lids tighten, upper lids narrow, pupils remain stable, nostrils move with controlled breathing, and the lips stay slightly parted without widening. A short swallow passes through the throat, then neck and shoulders re-lock. At the aiming beat she says in a low tight voice: <d>[English] Come on.</d> Use a brief subtle slow-down here, then return to normal speed. [Shot 3] At 00:04.200, the camera moves into a semi-circling medium close shot. She keeps moving along the roof, completing one continuous chain of knee-slide, reach, recoil, shoulder turn, and rifle pressure while debris crosses foreground. On the third trigger pull, the rifle clicks empty with a sharp dry mechanical snap. Her reaction is microscopic: the gaze tightens for a split second, pupils pull in, the brow center deepens slightly, one mouth corner draws back, and the jaw muscle locks. Her breath shifts into one shallower quicker inhale. She immediately folds behind the half-open hatch. [Shot 4] At 00:06.600, in a tight cause-and-effect shot, she half-crouches behind the hatch. Though the rifle is empty, she keeps the muzzle high to fake suppression. Then two dull knocks sound from below. The camera follows her gaze downward. A bloodied hand reaches out from the hatch gap, and an enemy identification strip is visible on the sleeve. Her reaction is not pity or shock but rapid reassessment: her eyes drop to the hand, sweep to the marker, then return to the gap. The upper lid lifts slightly then presses down again. The brow center smooths into colder decision. Lips move from slightly open to pressed shut, lower lip drawing inward. The throat moves once, followed by the smallest downward nod. She shifts to a one-handed rifle grip and yanks the hatch with her free hand. [Shot 5] At 00:09.000, continue the pseudo-one-take into the angled vehicle side. She hooks the rifle sling through the hatch handle, turns sideways, and slides down the armored side using her body weight to wrench the hatch while kicking a nearby burning metal fragment into a leaking fuel trail. Every element stays linked in one action chain: shoulders, back, waist, legs, sling, hatch, and fireline. Her face is fully task-driven now; eyes stay on landing points, jaw locks briefly, cheek muscles tighten, and nostrils widen on short forceful breaths. The fuel catches and runs into a fast near-ground wall of flame, dangerous and close but not a giant explosion. The camera drops with her slide, and flame crosses the lens to create a hidden transition. [Shot 6] At 00:12.000, move into a vehicle-side medium close shot near half-body range. The wall of fire briefly blocks enemy sight. She bends into the hatch, drags out a wounded young soldier, and with the same motion presses his head down against her side with her forearm while moving forward along the vehicle. Give one very short lethal glance back to confirm the fire is masking them. In that glance, side face, neck line, collarbone edge, and fire-glazed jacket highlight flash together. Her breath is visible but controlled. The eyes show not softness but hard protective command. The gaze sweeps back once and returns immediately. The lip line presses flat, then opens only enough to say quietly to the soldier: <d>[English] Stay low. Move.</d> “Stay” is low and steady, “low” lands heavier, “Move” is short and direct. Her mouth closes immediately after, the corners draw inward again, and breath exits sharply through the nose. End as she pulls the boy into smoke and orange reflections, with the frame swallowed by smoke and firelight, no black screen.

overall_soundscape: The sound field is driven by close weapon fire, the dry empty-chamber click, metal scraping, boots on armored steel, controlled breathing, hatch knocks, sling tension, fuel ignition, the low ground roar of a fast fireline, and distant blurred battlefield ambience. Keep the first half tight around palm-on-metal friction, roof slide scrape, sparks, rifle bursts, breath, heat, cloth tension, and the intimate pressed-through-teeth delivery of <d>[English] Come on.</d> After the empty click, let her breathing shift immediately. Reduce broad battle noise for the hatch knocks and the scrape of the reaching hand, then build the physical chain of hatch wrenching, boot impact on burning metal, ignition hiss turning into ground fire, body slide along armor, and the strain of pulling the wounded boy free. The final beat is carried by fire, smoke, steel, movement, and her low command <d>[English] Stay low. Move.</d>

non_diegetic_music: Use almost no score. If any music is present, keep it as a very low-frequency pressure bed entering mainly in the second half. Let body noise, weapon mechanics, metal, and flame dominate. As the empty click and the bloodied hand reframe the situation, introduce a restrained low pulse; deepen it slightly when the fuel line catches and the rescue begins, but never let it overpower breath, steel, or fire. End in unresolved forward tension rather than triumph.

## 29. 千と千尋の神隠しの有名なあのシーンを再現してみたかったので、MiniMax H3で試してみました。プロンプトは約10秒で設定してます。

</a>

<details>
<summary><strong>Prompt</strong> — Use Reference Image 1 as the exact starting frame. Preserve the entire screenshot faithfully, including the social media interface, profile information, embedded image, icons,...</summary>

~~~~text
Use Reference Image 1 as the exact starting frame.

Preserve the entire screenshot faithfully, including the social media interface, profile information, embedded image, icons, buttons, layout, colors, and all elements except the post text that will physically detach from the screen.

For the first 1.5 seconds, the entire screenshot remains completely static and unchanged.

Then the visible post text begins to glow very subtly.

The post text is physically attached to the surface of the screen. The original characters themselves begin to detach from the screen one by one and rise toward the viewer as solid three-dimensional letters.

CRITICAL EFFECT:
The original 2D text must disappear from its original position at the exact moment each character detaches and rises.

Do NOT leave the original text visible underneath the floating letters.
Do NOT create duplicate text.
Do NOT keep a second copy of the post text on the screen.

Each Japanese character should behave like a physical object being lifted from the screen:
the flat character on the screen disappears as it separates,
while the corresponding three-dimensional character continues moving upward into the space in front of the screen.

From 2 to 4 seconds, the characters gradually detach from the screen and float several centimeters in front of it. Their original Japanese shapes, wording, spacing, and typography remain recognizable.

The area where the original text was displayed becomes naturally blank after the characters leave. The rest of the social media interface remains unchanged.

From 4 to 6 seconds, the detached characters hover independently in front of the screen. They have subtle three-dimensional depth, soft shadows, delicate warm illumination, and realistic spatial separation from the flat screen.

From 6 to 8 seconds, the floating characters begin moving in a smooth circular orbit around an invisible central point. The characters gradually form a graceful spiral vortex.

From 8 to 9.5 seconds, the spiral tightens and the characters are pulled toward the center of the vortex. They rotate and move deeper into three-dimensional space.

At the end, the floating characters dissolve into a small number of subtle glowing particles and disappear.

The original text does NOT return.

The final frame shows the same social media screenshot with the original post-text area now empty, while every other part of the interface remains unchanged.

Keep the camera completely static. No zoom, pan, tilt, rotation, or reframing.

Do not animate the social media interface.
Do not animate the profile picture.
Do not animate the embedded image.
Do not animate the icons or buttons.
Do not create any new text.
Do not rewrite or replace the Japanese text.
Do not duplicate the Japanese text.

The only moving elements are the original post characters after they detach from the screen, followed by their spiral motion and disappearance.

The effect should look like real physical letters being peeled away from a digital screen and released into three-dimensional space. Elegant, mysterious, cinematic, restrained, and physically convincing.
~~~~

</details>

~~~~text
For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, <Picture 1> (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced.

integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] A 15-second, 16:9, dark epic war cinematic image-to-video sequence based on <Picture 1>, unfolding in one continuous snowfield frontline under a blizzard. Preserve the exact same female orc war leader throughout: same green skin, sharp face, long black hair wet with snow, heavy armor with fur collar and metal plates, and the same massive greatsword. Keep strict identity, face, armor, and weapon continuity. The distant black castle remains only background pressure and never becomes a new scene. No location switch, no giant ending wide shot, no battlefield overview, and no static posing. The tone is cold, brutal, oppressive, darkly sensual through invasive presence, wet hair, armor line, and ruthless command energy, never through posing. Wind and snow strike laterally throughout, frost clings to armor edges, breath appears as short white vapor, and the greatsword drags through snow with visible resistance, ice dust, and a sharp groove. At 00:00.000-00:03.000, the camera slides ultra-low above the snow through wind-driven grains, broken ice, and cracked crust. From the haze, the female orc leader drives into frame at speed. Her greatsword trails behind on her right, scraping the snow and cutting a long severe line. Her stride is heavy, stable, unstoppable. A few orc soldiers advance behind her in pressure formation, but she remains the focus. Her expression is hard-controlled: brow pressed down, gaze nailed forward, lips flat, jaw lightly clenched, nostrils moving subtly in the cold, breath short and heavy, with repeated white vapor from mouth and nose. [Shot 2] At 00:03.000, without cutting, the camera slides from her right-rear side into a near three-quarter side-face close shot while she keeps advancing. She sharply turns her head toward the formation behind and aside, wet hair and snow streaking across her cheek. Half her armor, neck line, collarbone edge, and sharp side profile enter frame together. Her eyes move first, then the head follows, showing precise command control. She throws one low short order: <d>[English] Move.</d> The line is cold, compressed, never shouted. A small burst of white breath leaves her lips. [Shot 3] At 00:05.000, with a violent but controlled wrist action, she flips the greatsword up from the dragging position and points it forward. Her gait shifts from heavy drive into a harder charge. The camera becomes a front-facing retreating track at medium-close distance. She is the spearhead. Behind her, soldiers ignite with her movement, raising shields and blades and roaring under pressure while snow erupts beneath repeated impacts. Her combat state hardens: gaze fully locked ahead, brow lower, eyes narrower, lips close then part briefly for a cold inhale, throat compresses, jawline tightens, and neck tendons rise. Emphasize sword-lift snow spray, real foot impact, exploding snow breakup, vibrating war gear, fur collar, leather, and armor edges. [Shot 4] At 00:08.000, still charging along the same frontline axis, the snow layer suddenly tears open beneath her path and reveals the partial body of a dead commander half-buried in snow. The camera dives with her motion over a frozen arm, broken chest plate, and packed snow, then lands on an old beast-bone clan emblem fixed to the chest armor, dusted with snow, grime, and frost. She does not fully stop. Her steps catch for half a beat, her body drops fast, and she rips the emblem from the corpse in one fierce exact movement. The emotion is subtle and brutal: eyes drop sharply, focus snaps close, brow center tightens for an instant, upper lids lift a fraction, lower lids lock, lips break from a sealed line into one tiny involuntary parting, nostrils twitch once, and the breath stops for half a beat. No tragic pause. The corpse remains only partial, frozen, and half-buried, with no horror emphasis. [Shot 5] At 00:11.000, the camera clings to her as she rises from the emblem grab, tilting upward from her hand to her face, then easing back half a step into a chest-up near shot, never widening into a battlefield climax. She clenches the old beast-bone emblem in one fist and surges upright while the blizzard hits her face head-on. Wet hair sticks across half her face. For one brief instant, pain needles across her expression: moisture flashes low in the eyes without forming tears, the inner brows pull slightly together while the outer brow stays hard, giving the feeling of hurt for one second without breaking. Her gaze drops to the emblem first, then rises forward, and at the lift of her eyes the emotion hard-cuts from pain into harsher battle intent. The upper lip lifts slightly, the lower lip tightens first and is then forced open by a roar driven from chest and throat. The mouth stays medium vertical, never too wide, never theatrical. At the same time, the greatsword sweeps across the front with her rise, her shoulder line drives forward, and she slams one step ahead as if cutting pain and rage together into the attack. The sword edge is just about to complete its strike, carrying an arc of snow powder across the near frame, when the clip cuts to black at the final instant before impact. No distant payoff, no army collision, only maximum near-character tension before release. Keep the performance micro-expressive and physical: cold pressure, command, ignition, memory stab, half-beat catch, pain flash, suppression, harder eruption. She must remain in motion throughout.

overall_soundscape: Build the scene from blizzard wind, heavy boots crushing snow, armor friction, greatsword dragging through snow, the sword being lifted and turned, and restrained roars from the orc soldiers behind her. At 00:00.000-00:03.000, emphasize lateral wind, blade drag, cracking snow crust, and her heavy cold breathing with repeated short white-vapor bursts. At 00:03.000-00:05.000, narrow the sound field around the head turn and command line; <d>[English] Move.</d> must land low, short, and hard with a small breath plume. At 00:05.000-00:08.000, intensify foot impact, shield and weapon movement, compressed war shouts, sword-lift weight, and snow erupting under repeated charges. At 00:08.000-00:11.000, sharpen inward for the dead commander reveal: rushing motion, the sudden catch in breath, the scrape of her drop, glove against frozen armor, the crack and wrench of taking the old beast-bone emblem, and a half-second interruption in breathing. At 00:11.000-00:15.000, restore full blizzard pressure against her face with hair and fur movement, armor strain, fist tightening around the emblem, the rise of the greatsword, a deeper inhale before the final roar, the compressed throat-driven roar, thicker white breath, and the snow-shearing sword pass, then cut sharply before impact.

non_diegetic_music: Use minimal dark war scoring, grounded and severe. Start with a low oppressive bass-and-drum pressure bed under the blizzard, never overpowering the physical sound design. Pull back slightly during the command line to leave room for voice and breath. As she raises the sword and the troops ignite, increase pulse and weight without becoming heroic. Thin the music for one half-beat when the emblem is revealed so the emotional stab lands through breath and tactile sound. In the final rise and roar, bring back a harder low-end surge that stops before full release, leaving the ending on suspended violence rather than completed victory.
~~~~

"Create a 15-second cinematic, photorealistic continuous-shot video based exactly on the reference image of “The Floating Night Market.”

Preserve the original architecture, floating wooden market platforms, lantern designs, vendors, customers, boats, ocean, mountains, color palette, and overall composition. Do not redesign or replace any major elements.

0–3 seconds: Begin with a slow, smooth low-angle camera movement just above the dark ocean surface, approaching the floating night market. Hundreds of warm glowing paper lanterns gently sway in the night breeze. Their golden reflections shimmer naturally across the moving water. Small waves softly move around the floating platforms.

Vendors naturally prepare food at small wooden stalls, gently stirring steaming pots and grilling food. Thin realistic steam rises into the lantern light. Customers walk casually across narrow wooden bridges, talking and browsing. Lanterns flicker subtly and sway with the wind.

7–11 seconds: Continue the same uninterrupted camera movement with a gentle cinematic arc around the market. Small traditional boats slowly pass underneath the floating platforms. Water ripples spread behind them. Food stalls glow with warm orange and amber light while the cool blue moonlight illuminates the surrounding ocean. The huge full moon remains visible in the background behind misty mountains.

11–15 seconds: Slowly pull upward and backward into a wider cinematic reveal, showing the entire floating night market surrounded by the vast ocean. Hundreds of lanterns form a glowing trail across the water. Reflections stretch and break naturally with the waves. Mist gently drifts around the distant mountains as the moonlight creates a magical atmosphere. End on a beautiful wide establishing shot.

Visual style: ultra-photorealistic, cinematic fantasy, realistic human movement, realistic ocean and water physics, detailed wooden textures, natural lantern glow, volumetric moonlight, subtle atmospheric fog, realistic steam, shallow depth of field during close shots, cinematic 35mm photography, high dynamic range, extremely detailed, immersive scale, smooth professional camera movement.

Motion: natural human gestures, subtle wind movement, gently swaying lanterns, flowing steam, moving water, realistic boat movement, no exaggerated animation.

Camera: smooth stabilized cinematic camera, slow controlled movement, natural depth of field, gradual push-in → forward tracking → gentle arc → wide pull-back. One continuous shot with no cuts.

Negative prompt: no scene changes, no cuts, no jump cuts, no camera shake, no distorted faces, no duplicated people, no warped buildings.

~~~~text
LEVEL UP YOURSELF — Retro Pixel-Art Arcade World

15-second, 16:9 high-energy 16-bit pixel-art motion-graphics film. 1990s arcade aesthetic, limited neon palette, chunky pixels, CRT scanline accents, sprite animation, parallax platforming environments, exaggerated game UI motion. Never photorealistic.

SUBJECT / MOTIFS: An ordinary Level-1 character gains tiny “identity pixels” whenever they complete a simple positive action. There is no instant transformation; the sprite evolves only after many repetitions.

TEXT LEDGER — only readable text:
“EVERY ACTION = 1 VOTE”
“+1”
“+1”
“+1”
“THE EVIDENCE BUILDS”
“IDENTITY UPDATED”

VISUAL CAUSALITY LAW: Every completed action awards exactly one glowing pixel. Pixels accumulate visibly around the character and eventually redraw their sprite.

CHOREOGRAPHY — 8 shots:
0:00–0:01.5 — Arcade boot-up burst. Tiny neutral sprite stands center screen. Giant pixel text glitches in: “EVERY ACTION = 1 VOTE”.
0:01.5–0:03 — Character opens a pixel book. Bleep! One gold square pops out: “+1”. Camera chases it upward.
0:03–0:04.5 — Match-cut: sprite jumps onto treadmill platform. One running step triggers another “+1”.
0:04.5–0:06 — Character rejects a giant flashing distraction button and walks past it. Third “+1” shoots into a meter.
0:06–0:07.7 — Meter is still almost empty. Camera zooms inside one lonely pixel, emphasizing that one action is tiny.
0:07.7–0:09.8 — Rapid 12-frame action cascade: read, run, create, clean, practice, sleep; dozens of +1 pixels stream into the meter.
0:09.8–0:12 — Meter bursts open; pixels swarm around the character, redrawing posture, clothing and aura one square at a time. Text: “THE EVIDENCE BUILDS”.
0:12–0:15 — Character steps through a pixel portal. New stronger sprite lands in hero pose while UI stamps: “IDENTITY UPDATED”. Camera crash-zooms into one glowing pixel.

A kinetic, artistic 15-second dark-fantasy trailer for THE STONE GARDEN. A young man steals forbidden fruit to free his beloved, awakening the garden's stone god. Ten cinematic animated shots followed by one title card. A rapid opening montage expands into readable two-second action and revelation beats. Animate bodies, fabric and foliage with physical depth while retaining the painted style.

0-1s: Close-up through blue leaves, a brief push toward the captive's frightened face. She reaches toward the rescuer offscreen left and whispers, "Please." Her voice is breathy, with a distinct upper-class British English accent. One fragile string note. Cut on her reaching gesture.

1-2s: Extreme close-up of her outstretched wrist. A living blue vine coils tight and jerks her hand back toward the foliage. Its leaves scrape her sleeve. Hard cut on the sudden recoil.

2-3s: Macro side view of the forbidden red fruit cradled in the rescuer's hands, still attached to a low red-leafed branch. He twists and pulls; show the stem snapping and the fruit coming free. The amplified snap kills the music. Cut on the break.

3-4s: Low medium shot tracking beside the rescuer as he clutches that detached fruit to his chest and bursts through hanging red branches toward the captive's clearing, screen right. Branches whip behind him. Cut as he clears the foliage.

4-5s: Overhead insert into the keeper's pot, securely supported by both hands. Its purple flowers abruptly wilt, their stems buckling inward as a magical consequence of the theft. A dry floral crackle. Cut on the collapse.

5-6s: Tight frontal close-up of the keeper's sunglasses and clenched jaw. He snaps his head toward the thief's route; a brief lateral camera slide catches the rose wall reflected in his glasses. A low bass pulse begins. Hard reaction cut.

6-8s: Low wide lateral tracking shot along a narrow passage through the rose field. The hooded hunter launches into a sprint toward screen right, following the rescuer's route. Roses streak past the foreground; his patterned coat trails behind him. Heavy percussion erupts with his first running strides. Cut during the pursuit, before he catches up.

8-10s: Three-quarter medium close-up of the seer. Her raven pushes off her raised hand as she looks toward the disturbance. A quick upward tilt follows its wings; her feathered head ornament stays attached. In a low, ominous voice with a pronounced aristocratic British English accent, precise consonants and non-rhotic delivery, she says, "Save her..." Cut at 10s, carrying her voice across the edit.

10-12s: Tight side-on rescue shot: the young man is left, the captive right, separated by vines. He reaches the same stolen fruit through a gap. She closes her hand over his on the fruit; at contact, the vines loosen and uncoil from her wrist. A short push keeps their joining hands and the releasing vine visible together. He still supports the fruit. The seer's offscreen warning completes, "...and you wake him." Cut on the release.

12-14s: Low-angle wide shot reveals the monumental stone god above the clearing, with the reunited pair small beneath it. A forceful push toward the god accompanies its red eyes flaring and its heavy carved head turning down toward them for the first time. It remains stone. Grinding rock overwhelms the score. Smash cut to black at 14s before it attacks.

14-15s: Hold a static title card for the full final second: THE STONE GARDEN, exact spelling, large centered coral-red uppercase serif lettering on deep ultramarine-black. The complete title appears immediately, with a single resonant impact and a short decaying tail. No other text.

Sharp cuts, urgent natural-speed action, readable hands and reactions. Preserve the rescuer's rightward travel, the hunter's ongoing pursuit and the stolen fruit's identity. The lovers remain together holding the fruit in the final reveal. Keep music beneath the dialogue. Both spoken lines are English with deliberate British accents. No subtitles, additional narration or slow motion. The final film title is the only onscreen text.

~~~~text
subject_definitions
<Subject 1> is the drawn boxer whose appearance, black outlines, white shorts with red accents, and spatial relationship with the physical tomato come from <Picture 1>.
<Subject 2> is the physical red tomato placed on the white paper, whose lighting and contact shadow come from <Picture 1> and <Picture 2>.
<Picture 1> is the image , used as a reference image for the composition anchor, character design, and layout.
<Picture 2>  used as a storyboard reference for the drawing progression and lighting.

summary
[reference generation] The target video is a single continuous extreme close-up shot where a physical red tomato on full-frame lightly textured white paper is incorporated into a live sketch of a boxer. The drawn boxer (<Subject 1>) comes to life, locks eyes with the lens, and punches the physical tomato (<Subject 2>), splattering it onto a locked perpendicular top-down camera.

retention_analysis
<Subject 1> (appears in [Shot 1]): fully_preserved - the boxer's design and spatial positioning are accurately maintained.
<Subject 2> (appears in [Shot 1]): fully_preserved - the physical tomato remains a static 3D prop until it is punched.
<Picture 1> ([Shot 1] composition anchor): partially_preserved - the exact spatial layout is maintained, but the drawn boxer will animate.
<Picture 2> ([Shot 1] storyboard reference): weak_reference - the sequence progression of drawing and lighting is adapted into a continuous fluid motion.

detailed_description
[Shot 1] A locked perpendicular top-down camera holds an extreme close-up view focused entirely on full-frame lightly textured white paper. There is no desk, no table, no visible paper edges, and no page corners anywhere in the frame. Under even studio lighting, <Subject 2>, a real three-dimensional red tomato, sits completely static on the paper, casting a soft contact shadow. A human hand holding a pencil enters the frame and rapidly sketches <Subject 1>, the body of a boxer, around the static tomato. The hand first draws black outlines, then switches to a red pencil for the shorts and the other glove. <Subject 2> remains entirely motionless, serving as the boxer's extended glove. The hand pulls completely out of the frame and does not return. The drawn boxer and the tomato remain motionless for a brief beat. Suddenly, <Subject 1> animates into motion. His jaw sets tight, brows pull down, and his eyes lock directly onto the camera lens in a dead-center gaze. Maintaining this unbroken eye contact, he shifts his weight and throws a heavy punch straight upward. This action launches <Subject 2> directly off the paper toward the lens. The tomato violently strikes the camera lens and bursts, with thick red juice, pulp, and seeds splattering across the glass, completely covering the frame and obscuring the view. There are no scene cuts throughout.

overall_soundscape
Near-silent room tone with a faint low bed under it. Rapid graphite scratching on textured paper as the hand draws. A sharp paper rustle and air whoosh when the boxer punches, ending with a heavy wet splat as the tomato hits the lens.

non_diegetic_music
None.
~~~~

subject_definitions:

summary:

Lisa gives mock sightseeing commentary while Alin drives, with dry humor and restrained old-friend reactions.

retention_analysis:
<Subject 1> (appears in [Shot 1], [Shot 3], [Shot 5], [Shot 6]): fully_preserved - Alin’s identity, clothes, proportions, and driver position remain fixed.
<Subject 2> (appears in [Shot 1], [Shot 2], [Shot 4], [Shot 5], [Shot 6]): fully_preserved - Lisa’s identity, clothes, proportions, and passenger position remain fixed.

<Subject 4> (appears throughout): fully_preserved - hood, cabin geometry, controls, worn materials, and seat orientation remain continuous.

detailed_description:
Photorealistic cinematic black comedy with natural timing, moderate-low saturation, soft pale-gold daylight, stable white balance, and no end-grade shift. Preserve natural skin, blonde hair, white clothing, blue denim, brown leather, and rust-red paint.

[Shot 1] The camera travels outside ahead of <Subject 4>, slightly above the hood and looking back through the windshield while matching the car’s speed. Keep part of the rust-red hood visible at all times, with small road vibration but never blocking the occupants. Through the glass, <Subject 1> stays in the driver seat and <Subject 2> stays in the passenger seat. Narrow margins around the car show <Subject 3>: road, wreckage, damaged buildings, and dust. The camera closes in slightly toward the passenger side but remains outside. Alin keeps relaxed road focus and small steering corrections. Lisa watches the ruins, then Alin, preparing to speak. No dialogue.

[Shot 2] At 00:02.500, the camera cuts inside to Lisa’s passenger-side medium close-up, matched on her raised pointing hand, with a ruined building still visible through the window. <Subject 2> (S1) says: <d>[English] Five-star hotel. Zero survivors.</d> First clause lightly tour-guide bright, second flat. She lowers her hand and looks to Alin. Alin keeps driving and does not interrupt.

[Shot 3] At 00:05.500, the camera cuts to Alin’s three-quarter profile, following Lisa’s waiting eyeline. <Subject 1> (S2) says: <d>[English] Your cooking?</d> Quiet, dry, and brief. Her eyes stay mostly on the road, hands still driving. Leave a short beat.

[Shot 4] At 00:06.700, the camera cuts to Lisa’s reaction close-up. <Subject 2> (S1) eases back slightly and says: <d>[English] Rude.</d> Clean single-word landing, then mock offense softens into amusement. Alin releases one brief amused breath. Keep the reaction small and natural.

[Shot 5] At 00:08.500, the camera cuts to an interior rear-seat medium-close two-shot keeping both women visible and seat positions locked. Ease slightly toward Lisa. <Subject 2> (S1) says: <d>[English] At least nobody asks about our five-year plan.</d> One connected conversational phrase, with slight emphasis on “five-year plan.” Her presenter tone relaxes into ordinary conversation. Alin gives one brief amused glance toward her, then returns to the road.

[Shot 6] At 00:11.500, the camera cuts to an interior lateral medium-close two-shot with Alin’s mouth and Lisa’s reaction both readable. Hold a short quiet beat. Around 00:12.000, <Subject 1> (S2) says: <d>[English] Finally. A world built for you.</d> Pause briefly after “Finally,” then land “for you” gently and precisely, finishing by about 00:14.000. Lisa looks back, lifts her chin, and gives one small mock-proud nod without speaking. Stay in this two-shot to 15.00 seconds: Alin keeps driving, Lisa settles with a faint smile, engine and cabin ambience remain. After the opening, do not cut outside again.

Use exactly these five dialogue lines in this order and wording only. Keep Lisa as S1 and Alin as S2 throughout. No extra dialogue, no repeated lines, no overlap, no voice swap, no listener mouth-sync. Motivate every cut through pointing, waiting, eyelines, and reactions. Preserve driver-passenger positions, window orientation, driving direction, plausible camera clearance, exterior parallax, real skin, layered hair, worn leather, rough metal, and moving shadows across faces and cabin. Exclude mirrored seats, outside ending shots, random axis changes, exaggerated acting, prolonged distraction from driving, glassy eyes, mechanical blinking, facial drift, malformed hands, plastic skin, orange cabin cast, blank windows, exposure pumping, and stationary backgrounds.

overall_soundscape:
Low engine, damaged-road tire noise, body rattles, and exterior wind continue throughout. The opening exterior sound transitions naturally into a more enclosed cabin sound at the first interior cut. Breaths and restrained amusement stay low and conversational; the last second keeps only engine, road, and cabin ambience.

non_diegetic_music:
N/A
